Kava moves to shut down lending platform after hackers drain nearly $4 million

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

HARD Lend plans to permanently disable new borrowing after attackers stole 53.9 million KAVA, leaving questions over how depositors will recover their funds.

Kava plans to retire HARD Lend's borrowing functionality after hackers stole 53.9 million KAVA, leaving the lending protocol facing a substantial liquidity shortfall.

The blockchain network confirmed on Nov. 16 that the Nov. 14 attack exploited a vulnerability in HARD Lend's accounting system, allowing attackers to withdraw existing KAVA tokens through manipulated collateral and liquidation transactions.

The stolen tokens were worth $3.7 million before the subsequent market selloff, according to blockchain analytics firm Bitquery.

Kava said it had patched the vulnerability and introduced safeguards to prevent attackers from repeating the exploit. It also accelerated plans to retire the lending service, with a governance proposal expected to permanently disable new lending and borrowing.

The proposal must pass a seven-day governance voting process, while existing protective measures remain in force.

“The planned retirement of HARD Lend's legacy lending functionality is being accelerated,” Kava said, adding that it would address remaining positions through an orderly process.

The company emphasized that the breach was confined to HARD Lend and did not compromise the underlying Kava blockchain or create additional tokens.

However, the incident has raised questions about how the protocol will settle outstanding obligations after losing almost all the tokens available in its lending pool.

Kava said attackers manipulated the value of BUSD collateral within HARD Lend, allowing them to repeatedly borrow KAVA and trigger liquidations that cleared their debts without recovering the borrowed tokens.

An investigation by blockchain analysis firm Bitquery found that the attackers borrowed about 55.2 million KAVA across 127 transactions and retained 53.9 million tokens after repayments involving other borrowers.

The attackers subsequently moved about 49.2 million KAVA to BNB Chain through LayerZero.

Bitquery traced the sale of 48.4 million KAVA, which generated approximately $2.09 million in BNB and USDT.

The investigation also identified a failed transaction involving 612,416 USDT through the Relay bridge. The service rejected the transfer with a “BLOCKED_WALLET” message, preventing the requested payout to Ethereum.

November 14 evidence describes three distinct access paths: lender claims, a failed bridge order and exchange transfers.

Lender withdrawals require usable KAVA in the pool. Blocking a bridge payout or reopening exchange transfers does not itself supply that cash.

Kava separately confirmed that part of the attackers' funds had been frozen and said it was working with exchanges, ecosystem partners, and law enforcement to trace and recover the remaining assets.

However, it did not disclose how much had been secured or whether the frozen funds included the USDT identified by Bitquery.

The recovery efforts have yet to resolve the more immediate problem facing HARD Lend's depositors.

Bitquery's Nov. 14 reconstruction showed that the lending pool owed users approximately 55.2 million KAVA while holding just 0.19 KAVA available for withdrawals.

The blockchain network said “Individual user deposits were largely unaffected,” but did not explain how that assessment relates to outstanding lender claims or depleted pool liquidity. It also promised further guidance for affected users as the retirement proposal progresses.

All 5 Versions Of Dungeon Crawler Carl, Ranked

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Dungeon Crawler Carl fans are waiting patiently for an update from author Matt Dinniman regarding Book 9, currently using the working title The Beautiful Place and expected to release in two massive volumes in the coming year or so. However, in the meantime, readers are filling their time going back through for a thorough re-read, which makes sense given how sprawling and somewhat complicated the story has become by the end of Book 8, A Parade of Horribles.

Fortunately, fans have a handful of ways to enjoy Dinniman's incredible story in addition to the original books themselves, including Jeff Hays' acclaimed audiobooks on Audible, the more dramatized Audio Immersion Tunnel experience, the WEBTOON and its print adaptations, as well as the newly released Dungeon Crawler Carl RPG.

Whether you're a newcomer to the series or preparing for a second (or third, or fourth) re-read ahead of Book 9, here are the best ways to experience the World Dungeon.

The Dungeon Crawler Carl RPG from Renegade Games Studios has already started arriving in players' hands who decided to back the crowdfunding campaign earlier this year, which hit a record-breaking $13 million in funding on Backerkit. The game is set to arrive in retail later this month, and it includes a unique adaptation of the first five floors of Carl and Donut's journey through the World Dungeon as a pre-made campaign.

That said, early reception among backers has been quite mixed so far, with players expressing concern over the quality of the product, a massively unorganized structure to the core RPG books, a half-baked gameplay system that could've used more time with play-testing, etc.

Beyond those issues, the RPG doesn't really serve as a complete Dungeon Crawler Carl experience, and it's hard to imagine anyone diving in without having read the original books already. As such, it's definitely not the primary way anyone interested in Dinniman's story should experience things, but it's a great option for veteran fans looking to immerse themselves in the universe in a new way.

If you haven't had a chance to check out the Dungeon Crawler Carl WEBTOON comics yet, they're easily the best visual adaptation of the series so far. With an official television series in the works, that's likely to change in the future, but for the time being, these are well worth checking out.

Launched in July 2025, the WEBTOON adaptation is published by Aethon Webcomics, adapted by writer Tevagah, and illustrated by Laurel Pursuit Studios, with Matt Dinniman himself consulted throughout the process. The full-color, vertically scrolling comic brings Carl and Princess Donut's adventures to life with vibrant artwork, over-the-top action sequences, and some fantastic interpretations of the books' most memorable characters and monsters.

However, while the WEBTOON series offers a great alternative option, not everyone is a fan of the anime-style artwork, and the series is also largely incomplete. So far, there are only two collected print volumes of the comic in circulation, covering the first 30 episodes and Carl and Donut's journey through the dungeon's first floor and toward the second. Meanwhile, the WEBTOON itself has only just finished adapting the original novel, with its early-access chapters reaching the end of the second floor, and including an exclusive epilogue.

The Dungeon Crawler Carl Audio Immersion Tunnel on Soundbooth Theater is arguably the most unique adaptation of the original story on offer. Unlike the traditional audiobooks, this production transforms Dinniman's novels into a fully realized audio drama, complete with a full cast of voice actors, original music, cinematic sound effects, and even additional scenes and bonus material approved by Dinniman himself.

Jeff Hays reprises his iconic roles as Carl, Princess Donut, and the dungeon's sadistic AI, joined by a talented cast that includes Andrea Parsneau, Travis Baldree, and Heath Miller. The result is something closer to a television series without the visuals, giving familiar moments from the books an entirely new level of immersion. So far, three complete seasons have been released, each adapting one of the original novels. Season 1 covers Dungeon Crawler Carl and the first two dungeon floors, Season 2 tackles Carl's Doomsday Scenario and the third floor, while Season 3 adapts The Dungeon Anarchist's Cookbook and its infamous Iron Tangle.

The third season wrapped up in June 2026, but there's still a long way to go, unfortunately. With eight books in the original series already published and only three adapted so far, it could be several more years before the Audio Immersion Tunnel catches up. Nevertheless, for fans looking to revisit Carl and Donut's adventures in a completely different way, it's easily one of the best options available.

Honestly, Jeff Hays' Audible versions of the Dungeon Crawler Carl series are almost as good as the original books themselves, and some would argue they're even better. Hays is equipped with an impressive range of voices, becoming inseparable from Princess Donut's voice to the point where he's even been cast for her role in the television adaptation. Beyond Donut, his distinctive interpretation of each character throughout the series truly brings the books to life.

In fact, there are certain moments in Hays' performance that take the text on the page to a completely different level and give listeners something that's not possible from just reading the books. One moment that comes to mind is Princess Donut's performance of "All Eyes On Me" in The Butcher's Masquerade, which on paper is a somewhat emotional moment but comes off more comedic than anything, whereas Hays actually sings the song and it's extremely powerful and has even brought some listeners to tears given the context.

Obviously, it's hard to beat Matt Dinniman's original novels when it comes to getting the definitive Dungeon Crawler Carl experience. What started as a relatively obscure web serial on Royal Road back in 2020 has since become one of the biggest success stories in modern fantasy, with millions of copies sold. It's an extraordinary achievement for a series that Dinniman initially began writing for fun, with no real expectations of commercial success.

Much of the books' appeal comes down to Dinniman's unconventional approach to storytelling and characterization, particularly when it comes to Carl himself. For all the absurdity of the intergalactic game show and ridiculous dungeon challenges, Carl is an extremely relatable protagonist. He's just an ordinary guy who's been thrown into an impossible situation, and Dinniman never lets us forget the very human side of a character facing very inhumane circumstances.

Carl's relationship with Donut is arguably the emotional heart of the entire series, balancing laugh-out-loud comedy with moments of genuine heartbreak. And despite the outrageous situations they find themselves in, there's something very grounded about their struggle to survive.

Probably the most impressive thing about Dungeon Crawler Carl, though, is just how easy the books are to devour. With the elaborate leveling systems, character builds, inventory management, complicated mechanics, you'd think reading them would feel like a chore, but somehow, Dinniman makes it all feel effortless. There's something to be said about a series that, by the time you've burned through another 600 pages, you're desperate to read more.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

17 SKI Editor-Approved Gifts For Hardcore Skiers

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

(Photo: Courtesy of DryGuy, Smith, Peak Performance, Hyperice, The North Face, Skida, Heather Hansman, Black Diamond)

Hardcore skiers can be difficult to shop for.

The more involved you become as a skier, the more specific your wants and needs get. Suddenly, the standard goggles no longer apply, and you choose your jacket not based on what looks cool but on what functions in the ways you need. If you have a dedicated skier in your life, it’s not always obvious what to gift them, especially since you’ll want them to use it and love it.

To help ease your mind, we’ve rounded up some of our favorite gift ideas for the people in our lives who eat, sleep, and breathe skiing: from powderhounds to corduroy snobs, this list has a little something for everyone. And we can guarantee it—we’re somewhat of ski hardos ourselves.

We all have that one friend who complains a little too much about being cold. But when your butt freezes the second you sit on the lift, can you blame them? That’s where insulated knickers come in. Throw them on over your base layers for extra protection your derrière didn’t know it needed. Wear them over your base layer for some extra warmth or on their own for a more loose-fitting base under your ski pants. These are the perfect gift for the skier you know who likes to get a lot of days in and stay nice and toasty on the hill. —Giorgia Menetre, Senior Social Media Strategist

Good goggles don’t need to cost a fortune. Dependable click-in, click-out interchangeable lenses are a staple for a reason—they’ll get you through just about every condition.  Smith’s OG reliable goggles offer a wide field of vision, ChromaPop lenses that make the view pop, and a flexible, comfortable frame. Lenses swap easily, with no worry of a magnetic slip-up. Don’t let another ski day go by letting the skier you love squint through bad light. —GM

These booties come with a high price tag, but they’ll be the gift hardcore skiers never forget. This updated compression boot from Normatec takes the pressurized, full-length boot system that has proven recovery patterns and adds heat. Dynamic air circulation throughout the boot’s treatment session helps muscles recover faster and reduces soreness. Remember how good it feels to put an achy and tired calf next to a hot tub jet? Now, imagine that level of recovery from the comfort of your couch. Boots come in three sizes and can fit a wide range of heights.

Please note that boots will ship the week of 11/23. —Jamie Aranoff, SKI Senior Editor

Hestra’s Fall Line mittens are consistently ranked as some of the warmest by our testers. The mittens have a cozy inner layer of brushed polyester and foam to keep hands insulated without overheating. The outside is leather that, once broken in, is soft and dexterous. Wrist straps ensure that the mittens stay with the skier, and not at the bottom of a chairlift. The mittens are on the pricier side, but will surely be a gift that skiers cherish for years. The same warmth also comes in a glove. —JA

Read our full review and browse other warm gloves and mittens here.

Vests are underrated heroes for skiers: they’re a great midlayer for touring and warmer days, easy to layer, and stylish enough to wear around. The Helium Utility down vest from Peak Performance checks all three of these boxes and more. The vest is incredibly lightweight and packs down into its pocket. More importantly, it’s windproof and water-repellent. A cinch at the bottom allows for customization for the right fit. —JA

If you’ve got an impossible-to-shop-for skier on your list, then look no further: this adventure camera from Nocs Provisions (yes, the same brand that makes binoculars) is sure to please. The camera is incredibly easy to use, even with thick gloves or mittens. Its simple one-button technology allows skiers to easily whip it out of a pocket and hit record (it takes both photos and videos). There’s no SD card needed, and content is easy to transfer to either a phone or computer with cords included. The camera takes retro-style photos and videos and comes with a lifetime “no matter what” policy. —JA

For any serious skier, Dermatone chapstick is the ultimate stocking stuffer. Not only will Dermatone protect lips from chapping, but the SPF keeps skin from burning. It also belongs in every first aid kit; it’s proven to help stop frostbite on extremities like lips, noses, and cheeks. Most ski resorts sell Dermatone somewhere in the shop, but here’s your chance to stock up and avoid inflated prices. Your lips, nose, cheeks, and just about everywhere else will thank you. —JA

Fleeces are always a solid gift, but Ridge Merino’s Alpha Wool Insulated jacket goes the extra mile. This fleece is designed to act as an outer layer for warmer days or uphilling, and a midlayer for when temperatures drop. What makes this fleece different is a mix of strategically placed wool to wick away moisture in places you sweat most, matched with a water-resistant Pertex Quantum Air that acts more like a shell than a fleece.  The fleece is complete with a hood, so no matter the weather, you’ll be prepared.—JA

The North Face just inked a deal with U.S. Ski & Snowboard to outfit the men’s and women’s alpine and freestyle teams for the next eight years. Bonus: They just released a version of the kit for the skiing public. The Comp Team Gore-Tex 2L Freeride and 3L Alpine jackets and pants come in alpine and freestyle designs, but the tech is the same: fully wind- and waterproof with plenty of features such as helmet-compatible hoods,  pit and thigh zips, and a multitude of smartly placed pockets. Don’t forget about the U.S. Ski Team crest on the right chest and all of the relevant sponsor badges to complete the look for your U.S. Ski Team superfan. —Samantha Berman, SKI Executive Editor

Here’s the perfect stocking stuffer for the uphiller on your list—or anyone who wants to add a little flair to their kit. Skida’s Pursuit Headband features the new Alpenbloom print, designed by U.S. Ski Teamer Paula Moltzan as part of the brand’s 2026–’27 collaboration with U.S. Ski & Snowboard. Beyond the athlete-designed pattern, the headband is a genuinely useful piece, made with a moisture-wicking, quick-drying fabric that regulates temperature without trapping too much heat and cozy fleece lining for comfort. A secure, nonslip fit keeps it in place whether you’re skiing, skinning, or working up a sweat on a cold-weather run. For guys, check out Ryan Cochran-Siegle’s Under the Lights print, which comes in a hat and a neckwarmer in addition to a headband.—SB

The Black Diamond Spark Mitts have been a tester favorite for years, and for good reason: you can’t go wrong with a short gauntlet mitt with a full leather outer. But the Spark goes beyond boring old performance. Every few seasons, Black Diamond drops a colorway with a fun, embroidered design on the inner side of the index finger. The Angel Collinson mustache model was our longtime favorite, but the current model with shark teeth is just as fun—and a good reminder that skiing isn’t so serious. —Jenny Wiegand, Outside Associate Gear Director

For the skier who skis in all conditions, this is the midlayer to give. At just 9.8 ounces, the Lightstream Full Zip stays unbulky while delivering serious warmth from -10 to 50 degrees. Vivo Extreme insulation is zoned for the job: 60 grams in the body and collar, 40 in the hood and sleeves, and 25 in the underarms and side seams. The 15-denier stretch nylon/PU ripstop shell is tough enough for daily abuse, and it also works as an outer layer for fall bike commutes and dog walks. Pair it with the Lightstream Insulated Short, made for skiers who run cold but won’t give up their shell pants, even on resort days. Full-length side zips let you take the shorts off when things heat up, without removing your ski pants. Light and packable, they’re also the ultimate emergency layer to stash in your boot bag. Available for men and women. —JW

Carv is the perfect gift for the skier who has everything, mostly because it’s the kind of gadget they’d struggle to justify buying for themselves. But if someone gave it to them? Game on. I’ll admit, I was skeptical of this digital ski coach, which uses sensors attached to your boots to analyze your technique and deliver real-time feedback. But after a few seasons, I’m hooked. It turns improving your skiing into a game, and it’s surprisingly addictive. Ted Ligety once told me his secret to topping the Carv leaderboard: find a stretch of groomer where you can make 10 perfect turns, then ski it over and over until you top the leaderboard. Personally, I might get distracted looking for powder, but I’ve found Carv makes even an ordinary groomer day more interesting. It’s fun, genuinely useful, and exactly the sort of gift someone might not know they wanted. Editor’s note: The list price includes a 12-month membership; continued coaching requires renewal. —Sierra Shafer, Editorial Director, Lifestyle

Few things in skiing are more universally useful than a Voilé Strap. These simple, stretchy polyurethane straps are the duct tape of the ski world, minus the sticky residue. Need to secure your skis together? Voilé Strap. Something breaks on your pack? Voilé Strap. Need to attach something to something else in a way the manufacturer probably didn’t intend? You get the idea. Made in Utah, these little problem-solvers come in a variety of lengths and colors and are practically indestructible. Every skier should have a couple tucked into their pack. Or, if you’re like me, scattered around the garage, the gear room, and the back of the car. You never know when you’ll need one, but trust me, you will. —SS

Why do we organize entire lives around sliding down snowy mountains? It’s a fair question, especially when you consider the expense, questionable career decisions, and occasional sleeping arrangements involved in chasing winter. In Powder Days, journalist Heather Hansman sets out to understand the people who’ve made skiing more than a hobby, exploring the history of ski-bum culture and what happens when the mountain towns that made it possible become increasingly difficult places to live. It’s funny, thoughtful, and at times uncomfortable in the best way, asking big questions about access, affordability, and the future of a sport we love. Give it to the skier who plans their life around winter, or anyone who’s ever seriously considered quitting their job to ski more. —SS

I didn’t win a single race in Alta’s town race series last winter, but I did win this boot dryer in the end-of-season raffle, and frankly, I count that as a victory. Wet ski boots aren’t just smelly. Moisture in your liners compromises insulation, making your feet cold even on mild days and potentially cutting a perfectly good ski day short. And if that moisture freezes overnight? Even worse. This compact dryer uses gentle heat and forced air to dry your boots without damaging their liners. What I love most is that it plugs into both a standard outlet and your car’s 12-volt adapter, so you can start drying your boots on the drive home. It’s also small enough to toss in your ski bag for a trip. Not glamorous, but genuinely one of the most useful things I own. —SS

I'm Glad Even Chris Pratt Is Talking Guardians Of The Galaxy Now James Gunn's Schedule Is Wide Open

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

An Internet User Just Made A Comment About James Gunn’s DC Tenure That Makes Me Feel Sad And Weird

Lanterns Showrunner Responds To James Gunn's DC Departure Amid Season 2 Questions

James Gunn’s Removal And Henry Cavill Talking Superman Has Fans Frazzled, But I Have A Hot Take

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Somebody check if Feige still has James Gunn's number.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I thought I'd made peace with saying goodbye to James Gunn's Guardians of the Galaxy. The third movie wrapped things up beautifully, and with Gunn running DC Studios, I figured there wasn't much chance of getting the old gang back together. Well, Hollywood has a funny way of changing plans. Gunn is leaving his leadership position at DC, and Chris Pratt is already talking about the possibility of making another Guardians movie with him. Let me just state for the record that I'm right there with him.

With Avengers: Doomsday approaching on the 2026 movie schedule, Pratt's future as Star-Lord has been on my mind lately. During a recent interview with MovieZine while promoting Way of the Warrior Kid, the actor was asked whether he'd be interested in reuniting with Gunn for a fourth installment. He hadn't spoken to the filmmaker since the DC news broke, but his answer was encouraging:

He directed all three movies, and that's pretty rare… so if he wanted to come back and bring it back, I would love that.

Well, now I want it too. Gunn spent nearly a decade building one of Marvel's strangest superhero teams into something audiences genuinely cared about. Somehow, he also made me cry over a talking raccoon more times than I'd care to admit.

This conversation is considerably less cheerful for DC fans. Gunn and Peter Safran are stepping down as co-heads of DC Studios following the Warner Bros. ownership shake-up, with former Marvel executive Jeremy Latcham taking over Dec. 1, raising plenty of questions about the DC Universe's future.

Gunn isn't exactly sitting at home waiting for Marvel to call. He's still involved with Man of Tomorrow, his follow-up to 2025's Superman, which is scheduled for July 2027. He also has other DC-related work on his plate. Still, stepping away from running an entire studio should eventually give him more freedom to choose what he wants to direct. But does he want to direct a fourth GotG?

Pratt's comments are especially interesting because he previously had some pretty specific thoughts about returning as Star-Lord. Last year, I covered his concerns about finding somebody who could fill Gunn's shoes. At the time, it seemed unlikely the filmmaker would ever be available. Suddenly, that possibility doesn't sound quite so ridiculous.

There's also the matter of where we left Peter Quill. At the end of Guardians of the Galaxy Vol. 3, he returned to Earth to reconnect with his grandfather, while Rocket took charge of a new Guardians lineup. The movie's closing credits promised Star-Lord would return, though the powers that be haven't said in what capacity or which new Marvel flick he might show up in.

Pratt recently confirmed there have been conversations about bringing him back, which is encouraging. Although returning for another Avengers movie and reuniting with Gunn for a fourth Guardians adventure would be two very different things. There's still no indication that Guardians of the Galaxy Vol. 4 is actually happening. Gunn spent years saying the third movie would conclude his trilogy, and he may have no interest in revisiting those characters. I certainly wouldn't blame him for wanting to move on from comic book and superhero fare to something completely different. At this point, he's been helming superhero content for more than a decade. Maybe he wants to spread his wings and take on a different type of movie, which I would be stoked for as well.

Still, I love the idea of Pratt and Gunn finding their way back to each other, especially after everything they accomplished with the first three movies. Maybe it's wishful thinking, but if Gunn ever decides he's ready for another trip into space, I hope Marvel answers the phone.

The first three original Guardians of the Galaxy films are available to stream with a Disney+ subscription.

Teledyne LeCroy Introduces Ultra Accelerator Link™ Validation System for AI and HPC Scale-Up Infrastructure

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Xena Z2244 UALink Traffic Generation and Error Injection solution accelerates validation of next-generation AI networks.

MILPITAS, Calif., Nov. 16, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Teledyne LeCroy, a business unit of Teledyne Technologies Incorporated (NYSE: TDY) and a worldwide leader in protocol test solutions, today announced the Xena Z2244 Ultra Accelerator Linkâ¢ (UALinkâ¢) Traffic Generator. Purpose-built for UALink, the system accelerates the development, interoperability testing, and deployment of next-generation AI and high-performance computing (HPC) scale-up infrastructure.

UALink is an open industry standard for AI scale-up fabrics, providing the high-bandwidth, low-latency interconnect required for efficient communication among GPUs, NPUs, and AI accelerators. As AI clusters grow in size and complexity, hyperscalers, cloud service providers, semiconductor companies, and system manufacturers face increasing challenges in validating performance, reliability, interoperability, and deterministic low-latency operation across accelerator fabrics. Built on extensive expertise in high-speed interconnect validation, including industry-leading protocol test solutions for PCI Express and Ethernet, the Xena Z2244 provides comprehensive validation of UALink implementations across 224G and 112G SerDes interfaces, with wire-speed traffic generation of up to 800 Gb/s per device. The platform enables engineering teams to confidently test their designs under realistic traffic conditions, identify performance bottlenecks, verify interoperability, and reduce deployment risk in large-scale AI environments.

"Our work with Teledyne LeCroy reflects Cadence's commitment to advancing the UALink ecosystem," said Arif Khan, Vice President of Product Marketing, Cadence. "The Xena Z2244 brings valuable traffic generation and error injection capabilities to UALink validation, enabling engineering teams to test interoperability, uncover performance bottlenecks and build confidence in their designs. By strengthening the tools available to developers, the Z2244 can help accelerate adoption and expand the market for UALink-based AI infrastructure."

Peptide Tracker Adds Washout Scheduling, Six-Month Schedule Preview, and a Redesigned Pulse in v3.2.0 and v3.3.0

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

New releases let users plan rest periods and dose changes months ahead, see their weight alongside their doses, and share weekly wins with the community

LOS ANGELES, Nov. 16, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Peptide Tracker, the free app for logging and managing peptide protocols, today released versions 3.2.0 and 3.3.0, introducing washout scheduling, a six-month schedule preview, and a redesigned Pulse that lines up a user's weight with the doses behind it.

With the updates, users can build rest periods into any protocol and see every dose day, washout, and titration step on a calendar before they save. Pulse now opens on a single large graph of weight or another metric, with a compound's doses lined up underneath, so users can see what happened in the weeks after a dose went up. In Connect, a new Weekly Wins feature lets users share milestones like "Took my first shot" or "Down a size."

"Our users don't run a protocol one day at a time. They plan cycles, rest periods, and dose changes months out," said Kevin Miller, Co-Founder and CEO of Peptide Tracker. "Now they can see the whole plan before they commit to it, then see how their body actually responded once they're on it."

Since launching in May 2026, Peptide Tracker has surpassed 100,000 downloads and maintains a 4.9-star average rating on the App Store.

Versions 3.2.0 and 3.3.0 are available now on the App Store, Google Play, and the web at peptidetracker.ai.

About Peptide Tracker: Peptide Tracker is a free iOS, Android, and web app that helps people log doses, manage protocols, set reminders, and track their progress over time. Founded in 2026 by Kevin Miller and JP Sio, Peptide Tracker is built by Flat Six Labs. Peptide Tracker is a tracking tool and does not provide medical advice.

Backlash Over ‘Robots’ Taking Photos at Christmas Lights Event

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Backlash Over ‘Robots’ Taking Photos at Christmas Lights Event

A video of Christmas lights being switched on in Central London has provoked a debate about people’s picture-taking habits and the importance of living in the moment.

The video was shared by BBC London on November 4. It shows a sea of people, seemingly every single one of them with their phones aloft, filming in anticipation of the elaborate lights on Carnaby Street turning on and the fireworks that followed.

The comments were flooded with people disgusted that no one appeared to be taking in the moment, instead watching a real event that was unfolding right in front of them through the small screen of their phone.

“I’ve stopped filming most things like fireworks and concerts when I realized I haven’t watched a single one of them back,” says Sufian Ahmed. “It’s so much about being in the moment. Experiencing it with the person you’re next to. You lose all that when watching it through a narrow screen.”

Many compared it to an episode of the Black Mirror TV show, while others called out the people for being “robots.” Others pointed out the hypocrisy of social media users complaining about the video while watching it on their phone.

Previous studies have found that taking a photo of an event affects human memory. A 2018 psychological study suggested that shooting photos or videos gives people a false sense that they preserved a moment, when in actual fact it’s the camera that did it for them.

In 2013, researchers asked one group of people to tour an art museum without taking photos, while another group was assigned to take photos. Those who took photos were significantly worse at identifying items in the museum and recalled fewer details about the items they did remember than those who didn’t take photos.

Dr. Kaitlyn Regehr of University College London and the University of Toronto says public attitudes toward smartphones and technology are shifting.

“This is not the first year that people have attended events and watched those events through screens,” the smartphone academic tells the BBC.

“But what’s significant is that this year there’s a lot of critical public discourse happening. And that, I think, is really, really positive.

Dr. Regehr says the backlash could be down to “our discomfort with the amount of influence that big tech is playing on our lives.”

Supreme Court rejects Custodia Bank’s bid for Fed master account

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

On Monday, the Supreme Court published its order list, revealing that it had denied certiorari in Custodia's case against the Federal Reserve Board.

The Supreme Court has said it would not take up a Custodia Bank's challenge to secure a Federal Reserve master account, denied its petitions for a writ of certiorari.

On Monday, the Supreme Court published its order list, revealing that it had denied certiorari in Custodia's case against the Federal Reserve Board. Custodia's legal journey has spanned over the past six years to try to obtain a master account, which allows institutions direct access to the Fed's payment systems and provides the most direct access to the U.S. money supply available to financial institutions. Those without master accounts are often forced to rely on partner banks with master accounts to provide services.

Custodia Bank, a Wyoming-chartered crypto-focused firm founded by Wall Street veteran Caitlin Long, began its master account quest in 2020 when it applied for that account in Kansas City. The Kansas City Fed then denied its application in January 2023, citing concerns about the bank's crypto-related business model. Custodia sued the Kansas City Fed in June 2022, initially over a 19-month processing delay and later arguing that federal law requires access for eligible institutions.

In March, an appeals court denied Custodia's petition for rehearing en banc, leaving intact a previous ruling that the Fed has discretion to approve or deny a master account for eligible institutions.

Custodia then petitioned for the high court to take its case up in July. The Supreme Court's decision to not take up the bank's challenged now leaves intact the previous decision. Custodia did not immediately respond to a request for comment from The Block.

Over the past year, attention has since shifted to an idea first floated by Fed Governor Christopher Waller for a "skinny master account." As innovations have evolved, the Fed said it may be time to offer a tailored approach while trying to mitigate risks, but some lawmakers have pushed back, questioning the safety and soundness of the Fed's payment rails.

The Fed asked for comments on the proposed slimmed-down master account last year and said it would not allow for interest on balances and could not access the discount window to borrow funds, among other limits. Comment letters from crypto advocates called the proposal "overdue" and also asked for some revisions. Community banks, however, warned that novel financial institutions were not subject to the same "robust regulatory compliance regime."

Custodia had previously told the Supreme Court that a limited purpose account was not sufficient, in part because it would mean that interest couldn't be obtained on the account's closing balance, Bloomberg reported.

Crypto exchange Kraken's banking division became the first crypto company to obtain a master account in March.

There are 10 patches of soil in the OoT Remake that require magic beans

Magic Beans in the Zelda Ocarina of Time Remake are used to grow beanstalks in soft soil around the game, and in so doing result in beanstalks in the future once you've travelled through time and become an adult. Magic beans in OoT basically serve as a way to prepare access to locations that you wouldn't be able to get to otherwise, at least in the future. Below I'll lay out how to get Magic Beans, how many you'll actually need, and all the soft soil locations so you can plant them in a single run.

There are 10 soft soil spots in Zelda Ocarina of Time you can plant in, so you should buy 10 magic beans if you want to use them all.

You can buy Magic Beans only from the vendor halfway along Zora River, once you've unlocked the Bombs after completing the Dodongo Cavern. He sells them to you for 10 Rupees, then increases the price by another 10 each time you buy them. He will automatically stop selling them once you reach 10.

Below is a list of all 10 soft soil locations in OoT where you can plant magic beans for beanstalks in the future (you cannot plant beans when Link is an adult). Keep in mind that you can also use bottled bugs to lure Gold Skulltulas out of the dirt too.

Once planted, beans eventually turn into plants in the future era that carry Link along predetermined routes, little hovering platforms. These allow you to reach previously inaccessible rewards and locations.

Knit Health raises $35m from NEA for Med-BEAM, a world model for healthcare

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Knit Health’s Med-BEAM predicts the care patients need next from billions of clinical decisions. Knit says it was up to four times more accurate and 250 times cheaper than frontier AI models.

Knit Health’s co-founders, from left: Jonas Knecht, co-CTO; Jonathan Kolstad, CEO; Maya Petersen, chief scientist; and Ted Robertson, COO.

Knit Health, a startup building an AI model that predicts what care patients will need next, has raised a $35m Series A led by NEA. Town Hall Ventures, SignalFire and existing investors joined the round, Knit Health said in a statement on Monday.

Chief executive Jonathan Kolstad first disclosed the round to Will Maddox at Axios Pro. The money will fund the rollout of Med-BEAM across health systems and other healthcare groups. Knit calls the model a world model for healthcare.

Scientists from the University of California, Berkeley founded Knit in 2025. It has raised $46.6m so far. Earlier seed and pre-seed backers include Frist Cressey Ventures, Uncork Capital, Moxxie Ventures, Coalition Operators and The Aligned Fund.

According to Knit, much of healthcare’s key information never makes it into notes. It sits in the orders, referrals and diagnoses that clinicians make, and in what happens next. That makes it hard for large language models to tell where a patient is heading, the company said.

Med-BEAM learns from billions of real healthcare decisions and hundreds of millions of patient records that follow people over time. It predicts the care a patient is likely to need and shows how different treatments could change their course.

Knit said it trains only on data it has clear, documented rights to use. All of that data is fully de-identified before it reaches the company. Med-BEAM checks its predictions against real clinical outcomes. That lets it flag when there is too little evidence to be confident.

“Healthcare still operates too often on what amounts to chance: which doctor you see, which day you arrive, which hospital you enter,” Kolstad said in the statement.

Knit compared Med-BEAM with a group of models from frontier AI labs. Each had to predict the clinical actions for hospitalised patients over the next 24 hours. Knit picked no outcome in advance.

Med-BEAM was up to four times more accurate, 100 times faster and 250 times cheaper than the models tested, Knit said. The company added that its model’s confidence matched real outcomes more closely.

Blake Wu, a partner at NEA, said one model could power “dozens of critical workflows”. He listed care management, patient navigation, clinical trial recruitment, specialist scheduling, hospital-at-home care and treatment-response prediction.

Jordan Belfort’s new AI voice startup, Tone, wants to sell like the ‘Wolf of Wall Street’

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Years ago, as questions mounted about WeWork’s soaring valuation, I sat down with co-founder Adam Neumann. We were negotiating a stage interview, and he asked to meet me twice in the weeks leading up to it, once at a WeWork in San Francisco and again at his office in New York.

I knew full well that the numbers that SoftBank in particular had assigned to WeWork made little sense, but I came away understanding why investors wanted to throw in their lot with him.

It’s similarly easy to understand why people are enchanted by Jordan Belfort, the self-proclaimed “Wolf of Wall Street.” Belfort famously pleaded guilty in 1999 to securities fraud and money laundering tied to Stratton Oakmont, the Long Island brokerage where salespeople pushed penny stocks in pump-and-dump schemes while Belfort acquired a Southampton mansion, a 167-foot yacht, and at least one helicopter. (He destroyed the latter two while high on Quaaludes and cocaine, as depicted in Martin Scorsese’s famous adaptation of Belfort’s autobiography, “The Wolf of Wall Street.”)

You might think someone who spent 22 months in prison and was ordered to pay $110.4 million in restitution to 1,513 victims wouldn’t get another shake. The victims ultimately received “far less” than they were owed, TMZ reported in July, noting that Belfort completed his court-ordered restitution obligations in late April of this year.

But Belfort, whose energy has a galvanizing quality, keeps resurfacing, including in recent years as a Miami-based sales consultant and one-time host of crypto workshops at his Miami Beach home. (A New York Times reporter attended a weekend workshop several years ago where the price of admission was one bitcoin or its equivalent value at the time of $40,000.)

Now, he is moving into the intersection of what he knows best — sales — and the hottest trend on the planet, AI, building out a voice-based AI platform. The secret sauce is that it imbues its voices with the kind of tonality that wins deals, he says, for example, by phrasing declarative statements as questions (which “builds engagement”) and even whispering at times (which “implies scarcity of information”).

Belfort says the platform, called Tone, is already being used by companies that have more sales leads than their human teams can handle. Now, in a move that could also help expand its training data, Tone is launching a self-service platform for content creators and authors who want to turn their books into “expressive AI-generated narration.” Users can select from Tone’s AI voices or, as a voice-cloning feature rolls out, create a clone of their own voice.

It’s easy to argue the market is already saturated with AI voice startups and technologies, and Tone comes with added risk: Belfort’s co-founder in the business is longtime investor Shervin Pishevar, who has a controversial past of his own. We also can’t independently verify that the technology works as described (neither co-founder is an engineer).

But if my time with Neumann taught me anything, it’s that doubts about the numbers rarely get in the way of a good pitch. When Belfort looks for investors to back him in this new venture, finding believers won’t be too hard.

Here is part of our conversation, edited for length and clarity.What have you built?

It’s a very unique way of going about building a voice, and it stems from a system of influence and persuasion I’ve been teaching since the late 1980s. It’s not just what you say, but how you say it. When you’re not in person, body language collapses into tonality. There are about 15 very powerful tonalities that people use all the time. The phrase I invented for this was “goal-oriented communication.” If I’m trying to be perceived as an expert, I apply tonality strategically.

It started that way. As a non-engineer, I went to first principles: How would I teach a human to do this? One tonality at a time. I have 30 years of super high-quality data , and I started teaching the model. It was a breakthrough moment, because it not only sounded exactly like me, it had nailed the tonality. Then I taught it a second one, and then how to link them. The key to all of this is data. Even the littlest bit of corrupt data dramatically negates the progress you’ve made.

How did you and Shervin Pishevar come together?

Shervin and I have been personal friends for a long time, and we were out for dinner one night. I had been pitched by an AI company that said it had an AI that could do selling. I listened to it. It was awful. But it put the idea in my head. I thought I was just going to teach models how to sell, but I couldn’t find a voice that was good enough. Necessity is the mother of invention, so we built our own.

Who are your customers, and how do they use it?

We have law firms and health insurance companies. We do a lot in polling and automobiles. It will take you through an entire health insurance sale. It asks qualifying questions, like what type of insurance you want, your budget, your ages. Then it chooses a plan and explains the features and benefits. Our latency is like 600 milliseconds. If the payment is too high, it’ll say it can bring the payment down and explain how to raise your deductible. If you don’t like the plan, it swaps you to a different one. It does literally the whole entire sale.

How does the AI handle a sales call that goes off script?

It never says “That’s a great question,” unless it really is. I hate that. It’s so ingratiating. In any sale there’s a happy path, how we’d want the perfect conversation to go, and every last word can be mapped out. But people have questions and concerns, and they take you off the straight line. So the trick is a model that can carry that perfect conversation, handle anything thrown at it, answer elegantly and return to the straight line, without breaking rapport and without hallucinating — or, as a salesperson, lying. We’ve trained the model to do that.

Have you generated any revenue from Tone yet? Have you raised funding for it?

Revenue, probably about a million bucks — $700,000, maybe. We’ve raised friends and family and seed, about $10 million.

I want to address the elephant in the room. You have a fraud conviction. Pishevar has faced misconduct allegations that he denies. Why should a bank or a hospital or a consumer brand trust this product coming from the two of you?

All I can say for myself is that I did make mistakes. It was a very, very long time ago, and I’ve spent the last 20-plus years teaching people all over the world. I’ve been hired by companies all over the world to teach sales and persuasion. If some companies don’t want to work with us, that’s their decision. But I think there would be more than enough that would see the value of what we created.

What close rate does Tone have versus a typical sales force?

I still don’t think it’s quite as good as a top producer. A top producer in the field would outdo it call for call. I think that will change in the next six months to a year. To make a model respond this quickly, you’re not using frontier models. We’re using our own very small, very light models, so they don’t have quite as much intelligence. I think it will significantly outperform a mediocre closer.

Where is it used most effectively? Low-dollar sales opportunities? I’d think the more money someone is spending, the more human contact they’d demand.

I don’t think it’s about replacing humans. What I see most with customers is that they simply don’t have the human capital available. They could get a lot more leads than they have people to call, or they need coverage at different times of day or in different languages. I think there’s always going to be a need for great human salespeople. I really believe that.

You’re competing with ElevenLabs and Cartesia and a lot of other voice AI companies. Tone is your edge?

I don’t think sounding human is really what this is about. They’ll all sound human in six months or a year. It’s the application of tonality that makes us different: the intelligence behind when it chooses a certain tonality. We have a patent on it. ElevenLabs is a great company, and Cartesia as well, but those voices didn’t work for us.

The FCC ruled in 2024 that AI voices fall under robocall rules under the Telephone Consumer Protection Act. How does outbound sales calling work?

We only call opt-in. You’re not going to use AI to cold call people. We would not allow that. You’d get sued by every TCPA lawyer in the world. It’s either opted-in leads or existing customers. The exception is polling, where there’s an exemption that lets you call private homes without opt-in.

How do you verify consent and prevent impersonation in voice cloning?

We could launch the cloning tomorrow. We didn’t. If you create a clone in our system, you speak into the computer, so we know who is creating it, and that person signs a consent. If someone wants to upload audio, which we don’t recommend, you’d have to match a live voiceprint in real time.

Who created the technology? You’re not an engineer, and Pishevar isn’t, either. I saw a name on the patent, Jordan Anthony Metzner.

No. Our VP of engineering is Ben Sussman. He’s a brilliant engineer. I’m the judge and the jury and the executioner of what sounds perfect. I’ll prototype something in the most obscene way, and my engineers rebuild it from scratch.

Do you charge per minute, or based on the outcomes customers see?

A combination. For streaming, it’s per minute. The content platform will be monthly subscriptions. And we have some clients where the bulk of our money is made through success.

Is this an independent company or something that belongs inside a larger AI company?

If the right offer came up, and it was substantial enough, and I thought it made sense, who wouldn’t entertain a conversation? But right now my plan is to raise a significant amount. I don’t think we need to own data centers, but we need enough reserve compute. I wouldn’t think I’d be selling it anytime soon, but who knows what the future brings.

Paramount+ released a documentary about you this summer. Did you watch it?

I didn’t. Believe it or not, I don’t watch or read anything about me, good or bad. I never read a comment. When you’re well-known, you have to live by who you are inside. I go by my own internal compass.

When you purchase through links in our articles, we may earn a small commission. This doesn’t affect our editorial independence.

Deloitte Digital’s new agentic tools aim to fix messy marketing workflows

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A new Converge by Front Office suite is designed to help brands better connect their existing technology stacks and wring greater value from them.

Marketers continue to wrestle with the gap between the promise of AI and the reality of the technology’s results, which for some haven’t lived up to the hype. Agencies and other marketing services providers are responding to persistent frustrations around AI fragmentation and execution with a wave of solutions themed around orchestration, or improving workflows.

Seventy-six percent of surveyed brand leaders believe their operating foundations do not meaningfully support realizing value from AI, which CMOs are under growing pressure to master, according to a recent Deloitte Digital report on personalization trends. Nearly all, 97%, of respondents say that the pace of AI development outstrips their ability to optimize AI for personalization purposes. Platform and workflow inefficiencies were also cited as hindrances.

The consultancy’s Converge Front Office suite seeks to address these shortcomings by bringing greater unity to marketers’ AI tech stacks. Agents will work to link existing tools and data, including from cloud-computing and software providers. Clients can also enlist Deloitte engineers to ensure Converge solutions cater to specific needs or adapt as their organization’s priorities evolve.

The Converge Marketing product is designed to break down silos and better integrate campaign strategy, planning, execution and measurement while ensuring marketers retain control over key decisions. Agent capabilities span research, strategy planning, asset building, campaign optimization and performance tracking. The goal is to get marketers from the pilot phase to production faster, Mark Singer, marketing service lead and CMO at Deloitte Digital, said in a statement.

Converge Sales boasts agents that can help identify prospects, engage inbound buyers and manage sales pipeline activity, among other functions. Deloitte Digital is aligning the new Converge brands with Converge Signals, a predictive intelligence platform that draws on data from partners including customer insights firm HundredX and Deloitte Digital’s own BrandWorth scoring system.

Other agencies are racing to hone their competitive edge on the agentic front. Publicis Groupe recently completed its acquisition of data-collaboration platform LiveRamp, a deal the ad-holding group says will enable it to produce more sophisticated agents on behalf of clients.

Deloitte has ramped up its agentic services as some of its consulting divisions experience a slowdown. The company’s technology and transformation unit grew 2.5% in the fiscal year 2026, a lower rate of growth than the prior year.

Ubisoft Massive Entertainment Boss Christoph Hartmann on His Amazon Games Exit and Turning ‘Splinter Cell’ Studio Into ‘A24 of Video Gaming’ (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Former Amazon Games chief Christoph Hartmann has been at the helm at Ubisoft‘s Massive Entertainment for five months and he’s now prepared to share his early vision for the future of the label behind Tom Clancy’s “The Division,” “Ghost Recon” and “Splinter Cell.”

Those staple Ubisoft franchises, along with new IP “March of Giants,” a multiplayer online battle arena game Ubisoft acquired from Amazon (where it was previously developed by Hartmann and his team), and an unannounced game, are Hartmann’s main focus and part of his overall strategy to turn Massive Entertainment into the “A24 of video gaming” one day. Related Stories 'Blue Eye Samurai' Sets Season 2 Release Date at Netflix Netflix Orders YA Drama 'Prep' Based on Curtis Sittenfeld Novel From Nicole Taylor

“I want us to be two things,” Hartmann says. “I want us to be definitely not just a shooter house, which we show with having a mobile, besides the other titles. I want us to be a label which really looks after the core gamer. And what I mean by that, it’s almost easier to describe what we’re not going to do. So I’m not going to do a racing game. I’m not going to do sports. I’m not going to do a casual kids title. But could we do an RPG one day? Could we do MMO? Could I maybe do even a strategy game? Sure, it’s all coming down to the right talent. If we have the talent internally or something happens, then we bring it on.”

See below for Variety‘s interview with Hartmann.

I worked basically in one company for 20 years, Take Two. I worked at Rockstar in the early days until “Grand Theft Auto: Vice City,” and then I formed 2K Games. But management was really self-taught. We knew how to make games and this and that. But growing the organization, I went to business school, but you don’t learn a lot about management, especially not the real world when you have to deal with humans and not just statistics. And I wanted to do something else and Amazon felt like a large company I can learn from how to do something. They wanted to focus on live services and I have a lot of experience with that and that’s why I joined them. And anything I really, really wanted to happen happened.

I learned so much about managing a large place, and I’m so grateful for it and it really helps me a lot. But it’s a while now, it’s seven years and at the end of the day, it’s a Big Tech company. And the tech industry is going through a massive transition right now. First of all, they have a lot of successful business they have to keep on running and then they have to focus on AI. Games just can’t be their prime focus right now. There’s so much going on. I’m just realistic about that. But for me, it’s the prime focus. I’m a content person. I’m not a tech person. I’m a content person. I love developing games. I love publishing games. That what gets me every day out of bed. I did that for 30 years and I’m just passionate about it. It means everything to me, and I felt I just need to go to a place again where it’s absolutely in the center of everything. And I think we have to be honest, Ubisoft definitely is one of those places. It’s still fully focused on games. I mean, they deeply, deeply care about it.

Some of my colleagues at Amazon are gonna read it, they’re gonna laugh. But they have certain things in the culture, I was like, why are we doing that? You know, there’s that writing culture and now as I’m out, I’m like, “Oh my god, it was so good” because it ended up in a games company. You have more than open-ended two, three hours, blah blah blah blah blah and you can actually get to the point? Can actually come someone prepared and tell me what’s going on? I can make a decision. And we start doing that … That’s by the way, the beauty of offering a smaller business unit like Massive Entertainment because it enables us. We have a few layers of management we don’t have, so we can actually talk to people on the team. And there’s so much to be learned. That’s one thing I like and believe smaller organizations, especially in entertainment, are just very, very good.

“March of Giants,” that’s a team that came over to join me. They’re all “Rainbow Six Siege” people, some of the biggest best talents they had there and I’m very proud to have built that studio and I love those people dearly. And I was not involved in the decision or the deal in any form because Amazon really wanted to focus more on third-party development, AI rather than so much internal studios, so they did it. But once I started having conversation with Ubisoft, which happened, by the way, in a very natural way. I mean, I started my career 30 years ago in Europe. They distributed every single game in France and Europe for us. I actually published “Rainbow Six” in Europe before Ubisoft did. It’s just a small industry, you know each other. I almost felt like one day it’s gonna happen, but obviously that was a huge plus. I’m like, oh my god, I actually get to finish the game because there’s nothing I hate more than not to finish what we started.

It gives me the opportunity have a set of titles, a set of IPs, which you fit together. It fills the right package together, and I can build a new business, but don’t have to start from zero. So it’s kind of a dream come true for me. It’s just a company which cares about games. It just breaths games every day. And and to the question I kind of heard in there what I’m planning for massive entertainment. What it is, because I mean, you know, we have “The Division,” “Ghost Recon” and “Splinter Cell,” the new IPs, “March of Giants,” then we have another one in development with some great people, but I can’t announce it yet.

And I want us to be two things. I want us to be definitely not just a shooter house, which we show with having a mobile, besides the other titles. I want us to be a label which really looks after the core gamer. And what I mean by that, it’s almost easier to describe what we’re not going to do. So I’m not going to do a racing game. I’m not going to do sports. I’m not going to do a casual kids title. But could we do an RPG one day? Could we do MMO? Could I maybe do even a strategy game? Sure, it’s all coming down to the right talent. If we have the talent internally or something happens, then we bring it on … Really my dream would be to become like the A24 of video gaming, like a place I describe with winning Oscars and still being commercially successful, definitely having impact on what they’re doing. It’s kind of the coolest place on planet Earth right now in you know a different industry, entertainment industry.

This interview has been edited and condensed.

Numa raises $45M to put AI agents to work in car dealerships

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

GM, Ford and Stellantis have opened their dealer networks to the startup's software.

Numa, which sells AI software that answers calls, books service visits and handles sales leads for car dealerships, has raised a $45M Series C led by Blue Cloud Ventures. It says more than 1,500 dealerships use it, and GM, FordDirect and Stellantis have approved it for their dealers.

Numa, a San Francisco startup that sells AI software to car dealerships, has raised a $45M Series C, the company announced on Monday. Blue Cloud Ventures led the round, with Threshold Ventures, Costanoa and Google’s Gradient Ventures taking part. Total funding now stands at $96M, after a $32M Series B in 2024.

Numa’s software answers dealership phone calls, texts and chats, books service visits and sends callers to the right staff. The company says more than 1,500 dealerships in the US and Canada use it. It claims to have handled over 1 billion conversations and booked more than 4 million appointments.

This year Numa pushed into sales. A new agent knows every car on the lot down to trim and mileage, and can suggest another if the one a buyer wanted has sold. In August, Numa bought SocketTime, which makes scheduling and repair tools for service shops.

“In two years, every general manager will have an AI COO,” said founder and CEO Tasso Roumeliotis.

He said the new money will pay for more engineers. Numa says it tripled its annual recurring revenue this year, but did not share figures or a valuation.

Carmakers are opening doors. GM has approved Numa as a vendor for its dealers, and more than 240 GM stores use it. In those stores, Numa says reply times fell from 23 hours to 24 minutes.

FordDirect, Ford’s dealer marketing venture, picked Numa as its AI vendor in August, giving it access to more than 3,000 Ford and Lincoln dealers. Numa is also approved for Stellantis dealers, about 3,000 more stores. Those are open doors, not signed customers.

Numa is not alone. Startups such as BadCo.AI are building AI for car buying, and voice AI firm Vapi raised a $50M Series B in May. Detroit’s three carmakers have also cut 20,000 office jobs since 2022, and AI is expected to speed that up.

Fetterman's Gaza-born challenger calls to 'abolish ICE,' oust top Democratic leaders

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Progressive Ahmed Moor is running to beat Sen. John Fetterman, D-Pa., in 2028, and is running in the opposite direction of the lawmaker’s current positions, including bucking the war in Iran and abolishing ICE.

FIRST ON FOX: It’s a circumstance almost ripped from a screenplay: a Gaza-born political newcomer wants to topple one of the Senate’s staunchest supporters of Israel in the Democratic Party.

But for Ahmed Moor, a member of the Democratic Socialists of America who is the first to toss his hat into the arena to challenge Sen. John Fetterman, D-Pa., the story doesn't end there.

"Look, so this race is not about John Fetterman," Moor told Fox News Digital in an interview. "John Fetterman's story is over, but the story of the United States continues. I'm getting in now because I believe we're in the midst of a constitutional crisis."

GAZA-BORN DEMOCRATIC SOCIALIST LAUNCHES PRIMARY BID AGAINST SEN JOHN FETTERMAN

Sen. John Fetterman, D-Pa., makes his way to votes in the U.S. Capitol on Sept. 30, 2026. (CQ-Roll Call, Inc. via Getty Images)

"I have some compassion for John Fetterman — I want to depersonalize," he continued. "Let's talk about policy. The policies that man has expressed and committed to are just a reflection of the special interests which have run his office for too long now. The problem of John Fetterman's policies is the problem of corruption in America."

Fetterman is up for re-election in the 2028 cycle and has become a target for Democrats in Pennsylvania ready to replace him as he continuously moved more to the center of the political spectrum and aligned with Republicans on key issues like the war in Iran, government funding and immigration.

Whether he runs again for a second term remains in the air. When asked by Fox News Digital if he had made a decision, Fetterman said, "The more, the merrier," to challengers entering the Democratic primary.

But Moor, so far, is the only candidate officially in the race.

FETTERMAN WARNS THERE IS NO LONGER 'COMMON GROUND' WITH FAR-LEFT DEMOCRATS OVER ISRAEL

"I know, he seems to really love Hamas," Fetterman said. "And now, so, like, so if you tell him, say thank you for running."

Moor was born in Gaza before immigrating to Philadelphia, where he went to public schools, attended the University of Pennsylvania and later went to Harvard to obtain a public policy degree.

His policy positions are in line with the growing progressive flank in the Senate Democratic caucus and echo the positions that Fetterman first ran on in 2022 when he pledged to support the Senate’s progressive standard-bearer, Sen. Bernie Sanders, I-Vt., if he won.

"I spent a lot of time on international policy, kind of a foreign policy specialist, one of the reasons the Senate job appeals to me," Moor said. "But equally, I'm committed to what I call the ‘Dignity Agenda,’ which is universal access to healthcare, childcare and affordable housing."

Among the top concerns for Moor in the "constitutional crisis" he sees in the country is freedom of speech, which he contended came under assault during the Biden administration when protests against "the genocide in Palestine" exploded across college campuses.

FETTERMAN PLAYS POLITICAL MAVERICK, BUT HIS VOTING RECORD TELLS ANOTHER STORY

Senate Minority Leader Chuck Schumer, D-N.Y., during a news conference following Senate Democrat policy luncheons at the U.S. Capitol in Washington, D.C., March 3, 2026. (Graeme Sloan/Bloomberg via Getty Images)

"Students were brutalized for assembly, for speech," Moor said. "That shouldn't happen in America. Donald Trump stepped through that breach and accelerated the assault. And ICE today is the worst expression, the violation of due process in America."

Like other Democrats and progressives, Moor wants to abolish Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE), but he would "like to see immigration law enforced."

"ICE is a lawless organization — ICE is a paramilitary militia. ICE is not law enforcement," he said. "Let's abolish ICE. Let's hold the bad actors who've killed Americans and kidnapped others in our streets. Let's hold them to account for breaking our local ordinance and our local laws. That's a criminal matter today in America. I hope local prosecutors run with it."

Ahmed Moor, 42, a member of the Democratic Socialists of America, announced that he will run for a U.S. Senate seat occupied by Sen. John Fetterman. (Ahmed Moor/Getty Images)

And like other progressives who scored stunning victories during the midterm elections to help flip the Senate, like Sens.-elect Abdul El-Sayed and Peggy Flanagan, Moor is against the old guard of Democratic leadership staying in charge.

It’s a position that is gaining steam in the Senate, where Sen. Chris Van Hollen, D-Md., is mounting a rare challenge to Senate Minority Leader Chuck Schumer, D-N.Y., to lead Democrats through the forthcoming majority in the upper chamber.

Moor said Schumer and House Minority Leader Hakeem Jeffries, D-N.Y., both have to go.

"Chuck Schumer and Hakeem Jeffries don't have what it takes to meet the moment. They need to be replaced," Moor said. "This campaign is also about sustaining pressure in that direction. If they survive the leadership contest, which is underway today, I'm going to continue to sustain the pressure on both of those men to do the right thing continuously."

Alex Miller is a writer for Fox News Digital covering the U.S. Senate.

Trump backs return of ex-Nvidia boss after 'Bring Jensen Back' campaign launched

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Donald Trump greeting Jensen Huang in September this year

US President Donald Trump has said he "would certainly not mind" seeing Jensen Huang handed back his job as allies of the former Nvidia chief executive called for him to be reinstated.

Huang resigned as boss of the AI chip giant last week after the emergence of two affairs, one of which was with a woman suspected of spying for China and another allegedly with one of his employees.

It has created a crisis for one of the world's most valuable firms, which is a key part of the AI industry and its huge expansion.

But now fellow tech bosses, including SpaceX's Elon Musk and Tim Sweeney, chief executive of Fortnite-maker Epic Games, have backed a campaign to get Nvidia's board to "Bring Jensen Back".

The pair were among more than 175,000 signatories of an open letter calling for the board to reinstate Huang while an ongoing internal probe is conducted.

President Trump gave his views on Huang's future on Monday, saying he "would certainly not mind" seeing the former boss given his job back.

"I guess maybe one lesson is more important than another, but I would certainly not mind seeing that," he told reporters as he left the White House.

The president's comments come after the "Stand with Jensen" campaign group, which claims to be made up of current and former Nvidia employees, partners, shareholders and supporters, demanded the return of the former chief executive.

They expressed concerns the ex-Nvidia boss "was removed without meaningful due process".

Huang had been expected to weather the storm following the revelation of an affair involving Alice Huang, alleged to be a Chinese spy.

However, he resigned last Thursday after a second extra-marital relationship emerged, allegedly with an executive at Nvidia who reported to him.

The scandal-hit tech boss is now discussing a possible return to Nvidia with confidants, according to the Financial Times., external

A representative for Jensen Huang declined to comment on the report.

The so-called war room also wants to get support for Huang from the White House.

President Trump has spoken in glowing terms about Huang since the scandal broke out, and also said he believed Huang when the former Nvidia boss told him he had "no idea she was a spy".

Trump's view is at odds with his Treasury Secretary, Scott Bessent, who has called for Nvidia to hire its next chief executive from outside the company.

The scandal has created tensions between the US Treasury and Commerce departments over shipments of high-end Nvidia chips to Alice Huang's former firm, Megaspeed. China has denied that Alice Huang is a spy.

It has raised questions about the possibility that the chips may have ended up in China despite US export restrictions.

On Monday, Trump said he did not know whether the women Huang had an affair with was a spy for China.

"They say she's a spy. I don't know that she was a spy at all. I mean, I really don't, I don't know that." he said.

The Trump administration issued new restrictions on chip exports to overseas data centres on Monday, which allow Chinese companies to access advanced AI chips to train AI models ahead of Trump's meeting with China President Xi Jingping.

The new rules will require companies in Singapore, Thailand, Malaysia, Indonesia and Brazil to apply for US export licences for the most advanced chips in an effort to cut down on them being illegally diverted.

Huang has agreed to testify in Congress, including before the House Select Committee on China to discuss whether US national security was compromised by his affair.

Trump said he did not think Huang had put national security at risk. "You know, China plays games with us. So we play games with China," he said.

The BBC understands that the likelihood of Huang being reinstated is low while Nvidia's internal investigations are ongoing.

The FBI is also investigating the affair between the former Nvidia boss and Alice Huang, according to Trump.

Musk claimed that "critical deals" involving Nvidia's technology would be lost following Huang's resignation.

"Jensen drove supply chain brilliantly at Nvidia but many of those deals were a handshake with Jensen, not a written contract," he posted on X., external

"Without Jensen, at least some of those critical deals will be lost."

He added later: "If Jensen gave me his word about compute delivery or I asked for components to be accelerated, he always came through."

Shareholders in Nvidia also want Huang back at the company's helm.

Hundreds of billions of pounds have been wiped off the firm's stock market value in recent days, but it is still worth some $3.87 trillion. Its share price was down in early Monday trading.

In an open letter to the board, Brad Gerstner, founder of Altimeter Capital and Atreides Management boss Gavin Baker called for management to leave "all options on the table, including the potential return of Jensen Huang".

Lord Sarfraz, a member of the UK's House of Lords, has also written to Nvidia's new chair, Harvey Jones, urging the firm's directors to give "serious consideration" to Huang's return following the investigation.

For the moment, Nvidia's chief financial officer Colette Kress is leading the firm and will present the company's results on Tuesday.

What channel is Switzerland v Scotland? TV and live stream for Nations League clash

📅 Nov 16, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A Scotland side still finding their feet under new head coach Sebastien Pocognoli conclude their Nations League B1 campaign on Monday night when they face group winners Switzerland in St Gallen.

Pocognoli earned the first home win of his tenure at the third attempt on Friday with a 2-1 win over North Macedonia but the unconvicing performance suggested the Belgian still has work to do to get the best out of his squad.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Switzerland have already secured automatic promotion to Nations League A after collecting 13 points from their five matches thus far, including a 3-0 win over 10-man Scotland at Hampden on September 29.

But while the hosts can relax, the game is not a dead rubber for Scotland, who can still claim a runners-up finish in the section with what would be a famous away victory over the side ranked 12th in the Fifa world rankings - 31 places above them.

The game is also hugely significant for captain Andy Robertson who will become Scotland’s most capped player, eclipsing Kenny Dalglish by reaching 103 appearances, if he plays on the night as expected.

Scotland will only finish second in Group B1 if they beat the Swiss and Slovenia fail to collect three points against North Macedonia in Skopje. Slovenia, on eight points, are one point ahead of Scotland in the standings but have a superior head-to-head record after their 2-1 win at Hampden last month that will see them claim the higher spot in the table if the sides finish on the same number of points.

Second place for Scotland would secure a Nations League promotion play-off in March against one of the third-placed teams from a Group A section. It would also guarantee pot two seeding for the Euro 2028 qualifying draw, which takes place next month. If Scotland finish third in Nations League B1, they will drop into pot three and face high-ranking teams in pots one and two.

The Nations League B1 fixture between Switzerland and Scotland takes place at Berit Sitterstadion in St. Gallen on Monday, November 16, 2026. Kick-off is 7.45pm.

The match will be broadcast live on BBC 2 and the BBC Scotland channel with coverage starting at 7.30pm.

The game can be live streamed for free on the BBC Sport website and the BBC iPlayer.

Scotland picked up no fresh injury concerns against North Macedonia on Friday but head coach Sebastien Pocognoli is expected to make changes. “I will need to consider the best approach for the game regarding the quality and also the fitness of some players,” he said. “We have some players in the squad who we have to manage. They cannot play two games in a row.”

Switzerland, meanwhile, are expected to rotate their squad. Head coach Murat Yakin will be without Internazionale defender Manuel Akanji, captain Granit Xhaka of Sunderland, Aston Villa's Johan Manzambi and Nottingham Forest winger Dan Ndoye. Yakin revealed that 20-year-old Sion striker Winsley Boteli will earn his fourth senior cap, while 23-year-old Young Boys midfielder Alvyn Sanches will be handed his seventh, both from the start.

The entire officiating team is from the Netherlands with referee Danny Makkelie taking charge on the field. He is joined by assistant referees Hessel Steegstra and Jan de Vries, with Alex Bos acting as the fourth official. Erwin Blank serves as the Video Assistant Referee (VAR), who will be joined in the VAR booth by Jeroen Manschot.

Switzerland 17/20, Draw 29/10, Scotland 7/2 - via oddschecker.com.
